# Zenbook ledger — every finding, ticked once

> Arch ledger for the ASUS Zenbook S 14 OLED UM5606W track (AMD Ryzen AI 9 HX 370 "Strix Point", x86_64, Radeon 890M,
> MediaTek MT7925 Wi-Fi 7, NVMe, USB4; its own 2880x1800 OLED panel is dead, so it runs on an external monitor).
> Cross-arch and shared-seam items live in `docs/dev/LEDGER.md` (ids `S<n>`, `P<n>`, and the seat-prefixed
> `SO`/`SP`/`SR`); rows below cross reference it by id. The rMBP's x86 findings live in `docs/dev/OS/rmbp-ledger.md`.
> Ids here are `Z<n>` — orin uses A-D, rmbp A/B/E, pi `PI<n>`, so a distinct prefix keeps the files mergeable without
> renumbering. The queue (the ORDER) is `docs/dev/OS/zenbook-queue.md`; the hardware sheet is
> `docs/dev/OS/01_BOOT_HAL/um5606w.md`.

> Rule (Peter, 2026-09-05): **an arc that fixes, flies, or drops a ledger item ticks it here in the same commit.**
> Peter, 2026-10-27: "begin support for the um5606w hardware". NO METAL until Peter says: nothing has booted on the
> Zenbook, and the first boot is the bench's.

> Created 2026-10-27 by ZENBOOKSURVEY (wave 36, cloud seat) with Z1. ZENBOOKNVME, ZENBOOKI8042, ZENBOOKGOP and
> ZENBOOKMT7925 each created the file in flight beside it and the seat unioned the copies at the fold; ZENBOOKLEDGERHEAD
> (Z39) restated the five preambles as this one, with every row below kept as written and in fold order.

Columns: `owner` = the lane whose file changes (`zenbook`, in ledger-check's owner set since Z39); `flies-on` = the bench
that scores it: x86 QEMU certifies QEMU only, the Zenbook bench is attended metal.
Status enum (GATE-LEDGER): **open** · **fixed-unflown** (in tree, not on metal) · **flown** (scored on the wire) ·
**landed** · **dropped** (ruled out, with the ruling) — free text after " — ".

| Z4 | **ZENBOOKGOP — the x86 UEFI loader picks the GOP OUTPUT (every handle enumerated, the first EDID-bearing non-internal one picked, `UNAOS_GOP=<h>:<m>` override) and the kernel says `[display] gop` at the scale latch.** Measured at 281b09c3e: the loader read only `locate_handle_buffer[0]` (`get_handle_for_protocol`, `unaos/crates/bootloader/src/main.rs:517`); the mode rule inside a handle was already EDID-native > current > largest linear (`:552-646`), kept. `mod gopsel` (file tail, x86-gated folds at `:517` and `:625`) prints `[boot] gop handle=<i> modes=<n> current=<w>x<h> edid=<yes/no> size_mm=<w>x<h> out=<int/ext/->` per handle (out = the ACPI _ADR display type) and `[boot] gop pick handle=<i>/<n> why=<knob/only/edid/console>`; one handle (QEMU) picks handle 0 as before. The BootInfo `size_mm` grant was declined: EDID-CARRY already feeds `font_core::ui::edid_ppi` through `video::dpi`, and the carried EDID is now the picked output's. `video/dpi.rs:104` folds `display_note`: `[display] gop <w>x<h> stride= format= ppi= src=<knob/edid/none> scale=` (OLED 2880 px at about 243 ppi -> 2.0). Measured, not changed: `desktop_uefi::activate` has one caller, the Kepler takeover (`unaos/crates/kernel/src/drivers/gpu/kepler_display.rs:511`), so the Zenbook runs the QEMU-shaped desktop path; WC is generic (`set_framebuffer_wc` from `unaos/crates/kernel/src/main.rs:112`). `unaos/arroyo` validates `UNAOS_GOP`. OWED: GOPIGNITE (a GopBoot `desktop_uefi::Origin` so a Kepler-less x86 runs `activate_on`); ZENBOOK-RADEON (DCN 3.5 scanout: mode set beyond GOP, the dead eDP disabled, hotplug); HIDPI-EXTERNAL (the external monitor's real ppi vs Peter's preference, a ruling); OLED10BIT (the 10-bit panel; GOP is 8-bit); FBWCLEAF (`set_framebuffer_wc` assumes BAR-only leaves; an APU carve-out in a RAM-shared 1 GiB leaf needs a split); GOPSERIAL (no UART on the Zenbook: the loader lines show only on the screen). | zenbook (video/loader) | Zenbook bench (first boot is the bench's); x86 QEMU for the one-handle wire | fixed-unflown | fold 32db4769b; code-only, uncompiled | ZENBOOKGOP |

| Z11 | **MT7925S2 — the MT7925 ladder's S2: driver-own and the chip id through the L1 remap, Zenbook only.** `drivers/mt7925.rs` file tail (`s2`, `own`, `map_page`, `read_chip`): ONE new state S_S2 that `wait_vol` hands to after the S1 line; only when `hw::platform()` reads ZenbookUm5606w. Driver-own is linux's CLR_OWN with OWN_SYNC polled to 0 (mt792x_core.c drv pmctrl; the arc's 'set-own' wording corrected), 4 tries x 50 ms = 200 ms; CHIPID 0x70010200 / REV 0x70010204 read through ONE HIF_REMAP_L1 (BAR0+0x155024) selector write and the BAR0+0x130000 window, the selector restored; firmware-own (SET_OWN) given back only when S2 took own, so the device is left as found. Linux reads the chip id before the WFSYS reset (mt7925/pci.c probe), so the reset stays S3's. Writes bounded: LPCTL at most 8, HIF_REMAP_L1 2. Wire: `[mt7925] s2 own=<ok/found> chip= rev= remap=<restored/stuck> rel=<fw/found/timeout> -> parked`, `[mt7925] own -> REFUSED lpctl= waited= rel=`, `[mt7925] s2 -> NOT ATTEMPTED why=`. Ladder renumbered: S3 = EMI + WFSYS + WFDMA, S4 = MCU patch + RAM + FW_START, S5..S10 kept. | Z8 | **ZENBOOKEC — the ACPI embedded controller TRANSPORT and a 256-byte RAM census (the SMC's job on the rMBP; the RAM layout is the DSDT's, unreadable without AML, so rung 1 is transport plus census, not battery numbers).** `drivers/ec.rs` (NEW, CHARTER Kernel — driver): the ports come from the ECDT only (EC_CONTROL GAS @36, EC_DATA GAS @48), located through the existing `acpi::root_sdt` + `acpi::find_table` (acpi.rs untouched) and decoded by `acpi_power::gas_io_port` (made `pub(crate)` on its own line, the seat's grant); no ECDT = no port I/O (`[ec] -> SKIP reason=no-ecdt`). RD_EC (0x80) plus the address byte is the whole write surface (WR_EC 0x81, burst 0x82/0x83 and query 0x84 are never issued); every IBF/OBF wait is bounded (`hw_wait_budget()/200` on `now_cycles`); a stale OBF at entry is drained and counted; the census stops at the first stuck handshake. `probe()` runs once at `pci.rs init` right after `hw::platform_say` (LINE-NEUTRAL) behind a new `hw::Probe::Ec` whose `gate` arm is the INVERSE of the Apple gate (`ec_runs`: true only on a positively identified non-Apple machine; the rMBP, QEMU and an unread SMBIOS print nothing). Wire: `[ec] ecdt=yes ports=data:<p>/cmd:<p> uid= gpe= id=` then eight `[ec] ram 0x00:` .. `0xe0:` lines then `[ec] census bytes=256 stale= ms= -> ok` (or `-> stuck at= step=ibf/obf status=`). `tests ec` runs the handshake against a fake port and the ECDT decode against a canned table: `:: EC: proto=ok cases=5/5 -> PASS ::`. power.md §1 SMC bullet carries the EC clause. Owed: ZENBOOK-EC-BATTERY (decode the census by the DSDT's EC Fields, needs the first boot's DSDT dump; a bounded EC conversation lock once there are repeated readers), the lid, the Fn keys (asus-wmi), `_Qxx` events (need AML and a GPE handler), the ACPI global lock (`_GLK`, AML), ZENBOOK-SUSPEND. | Z15 | **MT7925S3 — the MT7925 ladder's S3: EMI SLPPROT, the WFSYS reset, WFDMA0 to idle, ONE FWDL TX ring and ONE event RX ring, WFDMA0 enabled; no firmware.** `drivers/mt7925.rs`: ONE new state S_S3 that `s2` hands to only after a clean S2 line (selector restored, rel not timeout, CHIPID not 0xffffffff); the second file-tail block (`s3`, `wfsys`, `dma_off`, `ring`, `dma_on`, `give_back`, `spin_until`, `r32`/`w32`). Reused, not copied: `own` (take and give back), `map_page` (WFDMA0 0xd4000, DMASHDL 0xd6000, CONN_INFRA 0xf0000, the L1 window's second page); `read_chip`'s L1 select/push/restore hoisted into ONE `l1` with two callers (`read_chip`, the EMI_CTL 0x18011100 SLPPROT_EN write). Linux order (mt7925/pci.c:392-407, mt792x_dma.c): EMI SLPPROT; WFSYS_SW_RST_B (BAR0+0xf0140) low 50 ms, high, INIT_DONE polled at most 500 ms; `mt792x_dma_disable(force)` (GLO_CFG BAR0+0xd4208 EN/chain/omit cleared, BUSY polled at most 100 ms, EXT0 DMASHDL off, DMASHDL_SW_CONTROL bypass, RST LOGIC/DMASHDL_ALL pulsed); TX ring 16 (BAR0+0xd4400) and RX ring 0 (BAR0+0xd4500) base, count 32 and CPU index 0 written, base and count read back; descriptors 16 B from `alloc_zeroed` page-aligned (heap = bus address, Z2's shape), every ctrl DMA_DONE, refused before any ring register as `no-memory` or `dma-high` (at or past 4 GiB: one 32-bit base register, linux DMA_BIT_MASK(32)); `mt792x_dma_enable` up to the interrupts (10 prefetch words, RST_DTX/DRX_PTR ~0, PRI_DLY 0, GLO_CFG features then TX/RX_DMA_EN, EXT1 bit 28); own given back only when taken. No RX buffer handed, no TX posted, PCI bus master left off (no config write), so no DMA happens; no interrupt enable, no firmware, nothing transmitted (BP-net-7 kept). Wire: `[mt7925] s3 emi= remap= wfsys reset=ok/timeout waited= rst=` then `[mt7925] s3 wfdma tx= rx= cnt=32 rb=ok/bad glo= rel= -> parked` or `[mt7925] s3 wfdma -> NOT ATTEMPTED why=`; `[mt7925] s3 -> NOT ATTEMPTED why=unmapped/own`. Owed (not built): S4 (PCI bus master, own re-taken and the state after the firmware-own handoff checked, patch semaphore and sections on the FWDL ring, RAM code, FW_START, N9_RDY), S3b (WM ring 15, TX ring 0, RX data ring 2, interrupt priorities and enables), DMA-ALLOC-ONE (one kernel DMA allocator for the private alloc_zeroed copies in nvme.rs, ahci.rs, hda.rs, wifi tx.rs/rx.rs, mt7925.rs), S5..S10, HWROW-MT7925. Commands: legacy 64-byte mcu_txd on ring 15; FW_SCATTER raw on ring 16. Bus master: the ONE config write, COMMAND saved first and restored unless FW_START was acked AND N9 ready. Every DMA address checked below 4 GiB; the firmware Vec lives in `s4`'s frame and is dropped only after completion or after bus master is back off; no lock across MMIO, a spin or the FAT read. Two departures accepted by the seat: the WM ring 15 is armed here (every acked command rides it; S3b shrinks to TX0/RX2/IRQs/DUMMY_CR), and each scatter chunk waits on its ring-16 completion (100 ms) since linux gets no per-chunk event. One S4-only failure word beyond the design, `rb` (a ring read-back or GLO_CFG enable that did not hold). Wire: `[mt7925] s4 held= patch= ram= fw_start= n9= bm= rel= -> parked` or `[mt7925] s4 -> NOT ATTEMPTED why= bm= rel=`. | Z45 | **NVMEMSIX — one completion vector for the NVMe I/O CQ: MSI-X table entry 0 (else single-message MSI, else polled) on a `vectors::alloc`'d vector, the phase-bit poll kept as the authority.** Measured at ff4fa8a0b: `nvme::submit` polled the CQ phase bit inside `with_ctrl` (`block::hold::with`, interrupts masked); the I/O CQ was created `IEN=0`; the admin queue's only commands are `bring_up`'s four; `PciScanner::enable_msix` (drivers/pci.rs:247) programs entry 0 of a BAR0 table but maps nothing, and QEMU's nvme table sits past nvme's mapped 0x2000 window. Built: the I/O CQ created `IEN=1, IV=0` (entry 0, shared with the idle admin CQ); `nvme::arm_irq` after the last admin command: `PciScanner::msix_map_table` (new, appended cfg `nvme` at the tail of drivers/pci.rs) maps the table, `vectors::alloc("nvme", nvme_msi_handler)`, then the existing `enable_msix`, else `enable_msi`, else polled with `[nvme] irq msi why=` / `[nvme] irq polled why=`; the handler sets `CQ_PENDING` and counts `IRQS` (lock-free, EOI last); each poll spin swaps the hint off and the phase bit decides. No INTMS/INTMC write (NVMe 1.4 §3.1.3/§3.1.4 under MSI-X; one MSI message shares vector 0 with the I/O CQ; the seat accepted this departure from the arc's (b)). Under the masked hold the message latches in the LAPIC IRR and is delivered at the release: `irqs=` counts interrupts fired, not wake-ups. Every maskable ISR but the timer (ipi, xhci, xhci1, nic, ehci, i8042, nvme, ec sci, kepler vblank, dwi2c, tlb_ipi) is atomics and MMIO only; no ISR takes `CTRL` or `PORTS`. Owed, not built: PREEMPTOFF-SDHC, PREEMPTOFF-XHCILOAN, PREEMPTOFF-FAT, PREEMPTOFF-ARM (unchanged), UNAFS-READ-UNMASKED (this arc is its first step: the disk hold leaves the mask for an unmasked caller; the next is the MOUNT hold itself to preempt-off). | zenbook | QEMU `./arroyo test-zenbook` (`tests preemptaudit`, `tests preemptoff`, `tests nvme`, `tests unafslock`, `tests dongleboot`), then Zenbook metal | fixed-unflown — code-only, uncompiled | wire to read: `:: PREEMPTAUDIT: sites=178 isr=13 holder=138 both=27 converted=1 hold_irq_on=ok tick_in_hold=ok switched=0 -> PASS ::`; `tests nvme`, `tests unafslock`, `tests preemptoff` and `tests dongleboot` unchanged in verdict; never `[sched] preempt_enable refused` or `[sched] preempt-off leaked` | — |

| Z61 | **PREEMPTOFFSDHC — the sdhc driver's six CARD transfer spans run preempt-off with interrupts as the caller had them, no longer masked (PREEMPTOFF-SDHC, owed by PREEMPTOFFAUDIT Z57).** Measured at 0376ba7ba, built after the fast-forward to 8e4d8cace (same anchors). M1: the six spans (`drivers/sdhc.rs` :1510 `read_block_512`, :1677 `read_blocks_512_pio`, :2204 `read_blocks_512_adma`, :2611 `read_blocks_512` (CARD held across its 32 KiB `MB_MAX_BLOCKS` chunks), :2746 `write_block_512` and :3596 `write_blocks_512` (both `sdw`)) protect CARD (the controller MMIO, the ADMA2 bounce buffer and descriptors, the geometry); the SDHC vector is UNARMED by construction (`REG_INT_SIGNAL_EN` written at one site, :553 `arm_polled_status`, value 0; no vector, no MSI), so no ISR touches CARD (class II); nothing in sdhc.rs yields, sleeps or halts, so the count cannot leak; every card wait is `block::wait::bounded` on the TSC, so a holder switched out mid-transfer would resume past its bound having polled nothing and abort a live transfer: preempt-off, not merely IF-on, is the rung. The masked callers (the MOUNT holds through the UnaFS block adapter, the FAT spans) stay masked by their own span (the count nests); the unmasked caller is the UnaFS split read's `read_run` (MOUNT released), whose card runs now leave the mask. M2: each span is `preemptoff::scoped` around the file tail's `preempt_span` around the span's own `_held` body (one same-line fold each, before the line's first comment); the file tail's `preempt_span` says `[sdhc] transfer irq= preempt=` once a boot and records the armed audit task's reading (`audit_arm` / `audit_take`); `preemptoff::witness` (Z57's block) reads this CPU's count and task; `tests preemptaudit` gains a named sdhc leg (`sdhc_audit_leg`: a 64 KiB `read_blocks_512` from the unmasked test task; inside the span IF on and the count held; no switch across the read) and the line `converted_sdhc= sdhc_irq_on=`. The nine one-field CARD reads stay masked. BP-cores-23 and BP-storage-8 CHANGED. The arming order is already claimant-first: `yield_kat_arm` runs before the first window, `xhci_loan_yield_kat_arm` (block.rs) spawns `ptpyield-kat` on a sibling CPU, waits for handshake 1 and 2 ms more, so the claimant is in `claim_xhci_for_io`'s hlt-retry loop when the window (<= `LOAN_YIELD_WINDOW_MS` = 3 ms, at least two 1 kHz ticks) opens.
